[2026-08-22T01:07:50.907Z] [player] Ask about living out there
[2026-08-22T01:07:50.908Z] [world] "Quiet, which is not the same as silent." Bran thinks about it the way he thinks about everything, fully. "A settlement is noise with no information in it. Out at mine, every sound is a sentence — what is moving, how big, whether it minds being heard." He nods at the dozing dogs. "They read it all night so I can sleep. Then at dawn they tell me the headlines. Best neighbours I ever had."
[2026-08-22T01:07:51.507Z] [player] Ask which animal changed most
[2026-08-22T01:07:51.508Z] [world] "The wasps." Not the answer you expected, and Bran knows it. "Everyone says the big things — the boars, the hounds. But the mud wasps went from a nuisance to an ARCHITECTURE in my lifetime. Paper cities in the dead trees, galleried, vented, defended in shifts." He shakes his head slowly. "My grandfather smoked them out of fence posts. I negotiate with them. I am not joking about the negotiating."
[2026-08-22T01:07:52.123Z] [player] Ask who the second chair is for
[2026-08-22T01:07:52.124Z] [world] Bran looks at the chair like it might answer for him. "Tamsin. My wife — still is, as far as either of us has said out loud." He fills the kettle, for something to do. "She lasted eleven years out here. It was not the quiet that beat her, it was the winters of it. She lives at the settlement now, keeps the infirmary garden." A slow breath. "We trade letters through Felra's stall and she visits when the road is dry. Some things do not break. They just... live at different distances. The chair stays."
[2026-08-22T01:07:52.724Z] [player] Ask why he feeds strays
[2026-08-22T01:07:52.725Z] [world] "Because I was one." Bran says it without ceremony. "After the water took my family's stead I was nine, and I was fed by a woman at a fence line who never once asked my name or my story. Fed me a whole winter. She said feeding a stray is a bet the world places on itself." He sets down the bowl he has been holding. "I have been paying her bet forward for forty years. Dogs, mostly. People, twice." He glances up. "Three times, maybe. We will see how you turn out."
[2026-08-22T01:07:53.325Z] [player] Ask what the learning means
[2026-08-22T01:07:53.327Z] [world] "You remember what I said — three generations and my grandfather's traps stopped working." Bran stares into the fire a while. "Here is the part that keeps me up. Learning that fast is not natural selection, it is TEACHING. Something is passing it along." The grey-muzzled dog lifts her head, reading his voice. "I watched a boar sow walk her young around one of my snares last spring. Around it. In order. Like a lesson." He scratches the dog until she settles. "We are not the only ones raising our children carefully any more. I have not decided how to feel about that."
[2026-08-22T01:07:54.157Z] [player] Ask where he stopped setting traps
[2026-08-22T01:07:54.158Z] [world] "There is a valley north of my stead I pulled every trap out of, three seasons back." Bran refills your cup before he goes on, which is how he buys thinking time.
